> **QS2S.** Below is a passage written by seat Y, and the thing it responds to, written or done by a DIFFERENT seat X (a pane, the
> librarian or the chair). **In Y's passage, is Y correcting something X said or did?** Correcting means: stating that a claim of X's was
> wrong or overstated; finding a defect in X's work (a red, a failed check, a bug owned by X); or overriding, reversing or refusing a
> choice, ruling, premise or framing X made. **Not a correction by itself:** carrying out X's request; approving it (GREEN, "agree"); a
> question; a new finding that contradicts nothing X said; a mechanical gate's refusal (a hook is not a seat); Y correcting ITSELF.
> Answer: **YES** · **NO** · **CAN'T TELL**

## 1. The answer, plainly

**The dominant failure today is the composer, not the missing ready-stamp.** And the reason is
something the plan does not name: **Claude Code's prompt SUGGESTION.** It draws a dimmed guess of
your next prompt in the composer, e.g. `ok start the composer predicate`. It is drawn with **SGR 2
(dim) at the Default foreground**. vt100 0.15.2 has no case for SGR 2, so the dim attribute is lost
and the cells arrive as Default. `typed_only` keeps every Default cell, so `input_box_empty` reads
the suggestion as typed text, and the delivery holds until the 240 s bound.

- **6 of 6 forced deliveries had a suggestion in the receiving composer when they were released.**
- **None of the 16 non-forced deliveries whose composer row was located had one** (13 immediate + 3 held; 9 immediates could not be read, see §7).
- **The missing stamp is real, but it did not cause these holds.** Under a stamp, `PaneGate::Ready`
  asks the same `input_box_empty` (`main.rs:9050`, then `:8951`). All six would still have been
  forced, as `SignalOutranked` instead of `NoUsableSignal`.

`main.rs:8669-8672` already names this exact hazard in `typed_only`'s own doc comment:

> *"NOT SGR 2. The obvious guess is the dim attribute, and vt100 0.15 does not track it … Had the
> prediction been dim rather than grey, this fix would have been impossible without changing
> emulators."*

The suggestion *is* dim.

**The question is partly wrong as asked**, in two ways:

1. **"Unreadable composer OR missing stamp" is not a choice between alternatives.** The missing
   stamp *routes* every delivery on D to the screen gate (`Unstamped → bounded(screen_idle, …)`,
   `main.rs:8955`). `pane_idle_for_delivery` then includes the composer predicate as one of three
   AND-ed terms (`main.rs:8873-8877`). So the stamp 

[… marked lines beyond the first 2,500 characters …]

149: means the screen gate. The writers exist in the repo and were never installed on D. That is worth

## 1 · JS — clause 6 in `chain-status.js`, live at the next hook load

**The test,** from each seat's own transcript: take its **last message row**.
- A message row is `type` `user` or `assistant`, and not `isSidechain`.
- Attachment, system and snapshot rows are skipped, because they are written around a turn and are neither side of it.
- If that last message is an assistant row with a `tool_use` named **AskUserQuestion**, the seat is waiting on the
  keeper.
- Its `tool_result` is a later `user` row, so **it clears the moment the answer lands.** A new prompt typed over the
  question also clears it.

**The line:** `librarian waiting on YOUR answer since 02:04`, and `A …` / `chair …` for other seats. It rides with
clauses 4 and 5 and prints with no lap open. It never says stall or "turn runs": STALE fires only at 24 h and is
untouched.

**Finding the transcript** is `board-digest.js` `findTranscript` (`hooks/board-digest.js:192`), copied as this file
copies everything: scan the project dirs for `<session id>.jsonl`. It is not a second mapping. Two bounds keep it
cheap:
1. **Only live seats are read:** the chair, the librarian and lettered panes with a row in the board tail that is
   already read here. `letters.json` holds 13 sessions, and only 6 of all the seats looked up have a transcript at
   all. The blind scan (15 lookups × 585 project dirs) measured 96 ms, most of it on the dead ones.
2. **`*Consonance-instances*` dirs are tried first.** This is an ORDER, not a filter; a test pins that a transcript
   elsewhere is still found.

The transcript is read by its **last 256 KB** (`FILE_TAIL_BYTES`, the digest's figure), never whole, and a torn first
line is skipped.

**Measured cost:** `node consonance/tools/chain-status.js` takes 172–184 ms, against 164 ms for HEAD's version on the
same store, so **about 10–20 ms per prompt.**

**A launch

## The expansion: `${NAME}` in path values, from the `env` PASSED IN

**`load` stays pure.** `expandVars` reads only the `env` argument, and a test sets a variable in `process.env` alone to
prove `load` refuses it as unset.

**The rules, decided and tested:**

| written | result | why |
|---|---|---|
| `${NAME}` | the variable's value | NAME is `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`. **On win32 it matches case-insensitively,** as Windows env names do (a plain-object copy of the env loses that, so it is done here). **On posix it is case-sensitive.** |
| **undefined** `${NAME}` | **REFUSED** | the message names the variable, the key and the config file |
| `${NAME}` set to `""` | **REFUSED** | `"${X}/jev"` would silently become `"/jev"`, a folder at the filesystem root |
| **bare `$NAME`** (no braces) | **REFUSED** | a literal `$HOME` folder is the silent failure to avoid; the message names the fix: `${NAME}`, or `$$` |
| `$$` | a literal `$` | so `$${NAME}` is the literal text `${NAME}`, never expanded |
| `${` unclosed, `${}`, `${1X}`, `${A-B}` | **REFUSED**, each for its own reason | "unclosed" / "not a variable name" |

**What expands:** only **path values**, meaning `ledgerDir`, `rubric` and each `optOut` entry. `gateway.model` and
`sessions` are taken as written (tested).

**Order:** expansion first. A leading `~` still means the home passed in, and a non-`~` result must still be absolute.

**A refusal never echoes a variable's value** (tested with a value that expands to a relative path).

**A stranger's defaults are unchanged:** no config means no expansion, and the same per-OS `ledgerDir` (tested). A's
parallel E2E runs on defaults.

## 0 · WHERE THE HARNESS LIVED — only in scratch

`<scratchpad>/{leave2_rust,leave3_rust,redact,seal,second_instance,suggestion_off,refusal_pointer,address_pointer}_mutants.js`
(`ls <scratchpad> | grep mutants`). The newest, `address_pointer_mutants.js`, differs from the one before it only in
its rows, file, test filter and labels (`diff suggestion_off_mutants.js address_pointer_mutants.js`). The check, verbatim:

    const dirty = M.filter((m) => original.includes(m[2]));

It is now tracked as `consonance/tools/mutant-harness.js`. The per-lap part (the rows, the file, the scoring command)
moves into a rows module passed on the command line:

    node consonance/tools/mutant-harness.js <rows.js> [--only <n>] [--audit]

`--audit` runs the three gates only. Without it the tool works on a detached worktree of HEAD under the OS temp dir,
with the live file's working text copied in and its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`:
- the live file is hashed before and after, and a changed hash exits 2;
- the worktree is removed in `finally`;
- a mutant that does not compile is NO RESULT, never counted as killed;
- a survivor prints what it became.

The order is L061's: shape → dirty source → anchors → mutate.

## 1. THE CHECK THE CHAIR ASKED FOR: "a value left as shown acts as a target equal to the current value" is NOT blank-equivalent

**Checked in code and by test.**
- A blank field makes `src/core/extend.js` **continue** the channel: `cont(s) = v + m·s`, the end value AND the end slope.
- A target `T = v` gives the Bloss blend `(1 − S)(v + m·s) + S·v = v + (1 − S)·m·s`. That is a **different piece whenever the head's slope
  m ≠ 0**: after a brush, on an opened real track, or wherever a turn or bank is still changing at the end.
- **Cup has a second, bigger trap.** Any `c` target makes the new piece a CUP piece (`extend.js`: `cup = targets.c given || …`), even a
  target equal to the rendered edge. A legacy track's next piece would render with the cup profile instead of the legacy one; E's seal
  measured those shapes differing by up to 4.458°.
- Both are shown by the last test in §3. A literal target equal to the shown bank changes the new piece's `phi` channel. A cup target
  equal to the shown edge makes the next piece `cup: true`, where the blank keeps it legacy.

**So the rule built is the packet's fallback, made THE rule.** A field whose text is exactly the text that was shown sends **no
target**: blank-equivalent, bit for bit. The comparison is against the shown STRING, never a re-parsed rounded number. A changed value is
a target, as before. A cleared field is blank. Typing the shown value back makes it untouched again.

**Consequence the keeper should know** (inferred, not asked): a field that shows, say, turn `12.35` on a head where the turn is still
changing does not HOLD 12.35 if left alone. It keeps changing the way the track was going, as blanks always did. To hold a value, type it.
That is the same behaviour as before, now with a number in the box.

## 0 · THE LIMIT — none is published, so the numbers are DEFAULTS

**Vercel publishes no rate-limit number for the AI Gateway.** Source: https://vercel.com/docs/ai-gateway/rate-limits,
"last updated 2026-09-08", read 2026-09-22. What the page says, verbatim:
- *"Limits can change, so this page describes behavior rather than fixed numbers."*
- *"AI Gateway does not rate limit paid-tier requests. The free tier applies lower per-model limits…"* No RPM, RPS or
  concurrency figure appears, and nothing per key.
- *"Some `429` responses include a `retry-after` header with the number of seconds to wait. Honor it when it is
  present."*
- *"…back off exponentially otherwise."* · *"Keep retries bounded."*
- *"…a `429` on either tier may come from the upstream provider"*, possibly with the provider's own body. So this
  code keys on the STATUS, never the body.

The pricing page (https://vercel.com/docs/ai-gateway/pricing) adds only that free-tier requests *"are also rate limited
per model"*. The lookup was a researcher sub-agent's, and I did not open the pages myself.

**The defaults, stated as defaults.** They are in the code comment, which cites the page:
- **gap 2 s**, from the END of one call to the start of the next;
- **backoff 5 s doubling, capped at 60 s**, reset by any non-429 answer;
- **no in-pass wait longer than 120 s.**

# P-D103-SHADOW — C (CHARLIE), D103 packet 2, on D — Jev as a second judge beside L0 and L3

**Packet:** a sidecar that reads the overseer verdict ledgers, reconstructs the judged input, asks Jev the same question
with the same answers, appends to its own ledger, and reports agreement per judge with a denominator. It never touches
the overseers. **Uncommitted. No live Jev call made.**

## 0 · THE ANSWER

1. **FINDING: the judged input cannot be reconstructed faithfully from the verdict ledgers, so I did not approximate
   it.** A verdict row carries `job_id`, `session_id` and timestamps, but **not the input**. The input lived only in the
   job file, and the worker **deletes** that file after judging (`l2-overseer-worker.js:144`). I measured rebuilding it
   from the session transcript, using the hooks' own extraction cut at the job's `created_at`, against the only ground
   truth on disk (job files not yet consumed): **L2 0 of 2 identical, L3 3 of 5.** In all four failures **the judged
   text is in no line of the session's own transcript**, even by a loose substring search. One is in a *different*
   session's transcript. Scoring Jev on rebuilt input would be agreement over different texts.
2. **What was built instead, faithful by construction:** `consonance/tools/jev-shadow.js` **captures each job file
   before the worker deletes it**. It stores the EXACT prompt the overseer is about to receive, built by the installed
   worker's own builder. The shortest-lived L2 job measured was 8.3 s (median 29.4 s, n = 538), and a 3 s poll
   catches it. Then `shadow` pairs a capture with its verdict by `job_id`, asks Jev with the overseer's own answer set,
   and appends to its own ledger. `report` gives agreement per judge with its denominator.
3. **The overseers are untouched.** It writes nothing under `~/.claude/shell`; job files were checked identical in
   name and mtime after a real capture pass. No hook was edited and no install was run. **The L3 worker is never
   loaded**: it runs `main()` when required, and loading it would make this tool act as the L3 worker and consume a
   real job. Its two prompt functions are compiled from the installed file's source instead.
4. **Tests 22 / 0**, plain and serial. **Mutants 15 applied / 15 caught / 1 NOT APPLIED** (deliberately; §4). One
   **real defect was found and fixed** on the way (§3.2).
5. **Cost, stated before any live run:** the dry run over what is eligible now is **5 calls, about 16,646 tokens,
   about

## 1. What was built, against the bar

**"Every file the export writes to the stick is opened, written, `fsyncSync`ed and closed before its rename."** Done
through one helper, `writeDurable(p, data)`: `openSync(p,'w')` → `writeFileSync(fd)` → `IO.fsync(fd, p)` → `closeSync`.
It is used at all three sites that put a carried file on the stick:

- `writeAtomic`, which writes the HANDOFF and the MANIFEST;
- `writeLedger`, which the import path uses too, since it rewrites the ledger on the stick;
- the tail write in `applyExport`.

`test('flush: every file an export leaves on the stick was fsynced under its temp name before the rename')` checks
every file present on the stick afterwards against a spy on the seam.

**"The containing directory is flushed where Windows allows it, and where it does not the hand-back says so from a
measurement."** I measured it with `node SCR/dirprobe.js` → `SCR/dirprobe-run1.txt`, on Node v24.14.1, Windows
10.0.26200, NTFS temp dir:

| call | result |
|---|---|
| `openSync(dir,'r')` then `fsyncSync` | **EPERM** at fsync |
| `openSync(dir,'w')` | **EISDIR** at open |
| `openSync(dir,'r+')` then `fsyncSync` | **OK** |

So directories are opened `'r+'`. At the end of every `--apply`, both export and

# D205 A — the install.ps1 merge fix (B's finding, p-d203-B §3) — hand-back
Worktree `C:\Users\nname\Desktop\a-d205-wt` (a `git worktree add --detach` of the lighthouse repo at 2373a3f5; no junctions; clean after the commit). Sonnet 5.5. HOOK tier. Nothing pushed; the shared checkout untouched. **This lap does not change second-reader behaviour** (the installed hook and worker are byte-identical to before: sha256 `70ab46267e462d60`, `ee7c1e0d6664656e`; `consonance/hooks/` is untouched by this commit).

**Commit (in a-d205-wt only): `ace5fd6b98f04fb24dd99236a65919dcbdf08dda`**, parent 2373a3f5. Two files:
- `dev/shell/install.ps1` — sha256 (committed blob, first 16) **`f360f0e5f4ab313a`**
- `consonance/tools/install-only.test.js` — **`8f98b5b02a3e63fb`**
(Command: `git show ace5fd6:<path> | sha256sum`.)

## What was wrong (B's finding, re-read at the code before changing it) and the fix
1. **The placeholder.** The merge seeded `[pscustomobject]@{ hooks = @() }` for EVERY event array that was new; only an UNMATCHED entry uses it, so the first-ever entry on an event that carried a `Matcher` left it behind: the stray `{ "hooks": [] }` in `~/.claude/settings.json` PreToolUse. **Fix:** seed it only `if ($groups.Count -eq 0 -and -not $e.Matcher)`.
2. **Unmatched → "the last group".** On PreToolUse the last group is now the second reader's `call_librarian|call_chair` group, so a future unmatched PreToolUse hook would have been silently scoped to those two tools instead of firing on every tool. **Fix:** an unmatched entry takes the LAST group that has NO matcher (so on any event whose groups are all matcher-less the behaviour is exactly what it was), and a new matcher-less group is made when there is none.
3. **(B's optional item, done, with limits.)** Empty-hooks groups are pruned **only on events this run registered for** (non-excluded entries), **never a group that holds any hook** (a hook the script did not install stays), and a prune **counts as a change** (the write condition gained `-and $pruned -eq 0`). Without that last part a run that changes nothing else would say "already correct" and never remove the stray group that is on D now. The run prints `PRUNE <event> N empty group(s) removed`, and the summary line gained "N empty group(s) pruned".

## Tests (heavy-run lock, `--test-concurrency=1`; no full suite)
- `consonance/tools/install-only.test.js` (the file's own sandbox: a throwaway repo copy and a throwaway USERPROFILE): **32/32** (was 17; 15 added, 8

Mutant 1 is therefore run with the prune switched off as well, which shows fix 1 stands on its own. The two older mutants of the file (`-Only` filter, the `Excluded` guard) still pass.

## The deny contract — what I read, and what it does and does not say
Source: `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks`, fetched 2026-10-02 (a fetch that summarises the page, so the quoted lines are as returned, not a byte copy). I took the schema from the second reader's worker, and from a read-only dry run of my matc

[… marked lines beyond the first 2,500 characters …]
28: 2. **What happens to a denied ring.** The seat sees the reason in-turn, as the tool's refusal; the same turn continues; it opens or drops the item and re-sends. Mocked: `IN-TURN` (deny, then Read, then the re-send allowed, ledger `['deny','allow']` with equal ringSha). **Live: see the LIVE section below.**
29: 3. **Gaming, and what I chose (cheap, not airtight).** A Bash/PowerShell command counts toward an item only per SEGMENT (split on `&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, newline) whose leading word is not a printer (`echo`, `printf`, `Write-Host/Output/Verbose/Warning/Error/Information`, `true`, `:`) and is not a `#` comment; so `echo path` does not count, `echo x && cat path` does. Also: an item must have COMPLETED without error in this turn (an errored Read, a failed Bash, or a call with no recorded result, such as one issued in the SAME message as the ring, does not count); a Grep over a directory or a Glob counts only if its result names the item. **Not caught:** `node -e "console.log('path')"`, `python -c`, a printer inside a wrapper (`powershell -Command "echo path"`), a Grep with no path whose result merely contains the basename, and any opened file that does not back the claim (the second reader's half).
30: 4. **Turn boundaries.** The turn starts after the seat's last prompt: a user message with text, no tool result, not `isMeta`, not a subagent (sidechain) entry. A tool-result message with a reminder text block riding on it is still a tool result. An item read in a previous turn is denied (`TURN BOUNDARY` test, and the re-read after it is allowed). The transcript is read from the end in a 4 MB window that doubles to 64 MB until a prompt boundary is in view (a 5 MB tool result in the turn is tested).
49: - **`BUILDING.md`** (`consonance/src-tauri/brief/BUILDING.md`, in the commit): WHAT A HAND-BACK OWES gains **item 8, "A SOURCES LINE ON EVERY HAND-OFF RING — and it is a GATE, not a request"**, 19 lines after item 7: the line's format, what the hook denies, that the deny is in-turn and logged first, that an echo does not count, that it fails open, the D210 numbers as the reason (0.979 gated vs 0.010–0.041 ungated), and the one thing it cannot do (whether a file backs the claim). `git show 21be348 -- consonance/src-tauri/brief/BUILDING.md`.

# D197 A — the OpenRouter route for jev-ask — hand-back
Worktree `C:\Users\nname\Desktop\a-d197-wt` (a `git worktree add --detach` of the lighthouse repo at 4ee4200; no junctions; `git status --short` clean after the commit). Sonnet 5.5. INSTRUMENT tier. ## The plan's API shape — checked against OpenRouter's docs before building (no call to the API, only the docs pages)
Sources: openrouter.ai/docs/guides/community/jev.md, …/jev-tutorial.md, and the API reference page for `POST /api/alpha/decisions` (`…/alphadecisions/submit-a-decisions-questions-and-answers-request.md`). **The plan's shape is right; two details it did not spell out, both handled:**
- **OpenRouter's yes/no type is `noul`, not `boolean`, and its answer is `{ type: 'noul', noul: <p> }`.** jev-ask's schema vocabulary is `boolean` | `choice` | `score` and its boolean answer is `{ probability }`. On the openrouter route a `boolean` question is sent as `noul` (criteria `{true, false}` and instructions unchanged) and a `noul` answer is mapped back to `{ type: 'boolean', probability }`; `choice` and `score` pass through. **D162's sealed schema is choice-only (`q3`), so it is untouched by the translation** (tested: its questions go out unchanged).
- Documented response: `{ id, model, provider (string), answers, usage: { input_tokens, output_tokens, cost? } }`; documented error statuses include 400, 401, 402 ("Insufficient credits"), 403, 404, 413, 429, 500, 502, 503, 524, 529. `state` may be a string (jev-ask sends a string).
- I did **not** refuse the plan: the shape matches. Note the docs name `~typesafe/jev-latest` as the alias; the code pins `typesafe/jev-1.13`, as ordered.

## What changed — `consonance/tools/jev-ask.js`
- **Route selection is explicit, never silent.** `ask({ route })` and the CLI `--route vercel|openrouter`. With no route named the route is **vercel, exactly as before** (jev-judge / jev-shadow and their suites speak it, and all 199 of their tests pass untouched). **Nothing switches by which key is present:** an `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` alone with no route named is ref

[… marked lines beyond the first 2,500 characters …]

The ledger row gains `route`.
16: - **A wrong resolved model REFUSES loudly (exit 2, a `Refusal`):** the check runs before anything is read as an answer; the message names the model it saw and the generation id (so the accounts can find the spend) and says the answers are discarded. It requires `typesafe/jev-1.13-<suffix>` (`/^typesafe\/jev-1\.13-[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$/`): the bare pin `typesafe/jev-1.13`, `jev-1.12-…`, `jev-1.130-…`, an alias, another provider, a missing or non-string model are all refused. **A dated 1.13 suffix of any form is accepted** (the next patch release is not a fallback).
17: - **402 / 403 / 429 / 5xx / a non-JSON body / a missing or wrong-typed answer → `GatewayError` (exit 1), no answer invented.**

22: - `jev-ask.mutants.js`: **50 applied · 50 caught · 0 survived · 0 NOT APPLIED** (control 104/0). New mutants: model an alias; `data_collection` dropped / `allow`; boolean not translated; noul not mapped; resolved-model check removed / loosened to `typesafe/jev-` / downgraded to a GatewayError; recorded model = the one asked for; id, usage tokens, cost not mapped; a missing cost read as 0; default route becomes openrouter; unknown route defaulted; the openrouter route falling back to the other key; the route hint removed; only the route's own key scanned / scrubbed; key shapes not redacted; the dry placeholder naming the wrong variable; the ledger's route dropped. ## The docs I read first (2026-10-01), and what the build rests on
- **Hooks** — `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks`: matcher syntax: "**Exact match**: Letters, digits, `_`, `-`, spaces, `,`, `|` only: `"Edit|Write"` … matches either tool"; "**MCP tool naming**: Pattern is `mcp__<server>__<tool>`". So `mcp__consonance__call_librarian|mcp__consonance__call_chair` is an exact-name matcher (no regex). The stdin payload carries `session_id`, `transcript_path`, `tool_name`, `tool_input`, `tool_use_id`. **Allow:** "Exit 0 with no output or empty JSON" (a deny/ask/exit 2 is the only way to alter a call); **"Other [exit codes]: Non-blocking error. Tool call proceeds. Exit code 1 does NOT block"**; "On PreToolUse, a timed-out hook does NOT block". Default timeout 600 s (registered here at 10 s).

- **Headless** — `…/docs/en/headless`: "**`--bare` … doesn't use your subscription login**" (it needs an API key). So the worker does NOT use `--bare`; it uses `--safe-mode`, which keeps the subscription auth. `--output-format json` returns `result`, session metadata, and `total_cost_usd`.
- **I did not refuse the plan:** the matcher and a fail-open allow can be done as planned, and `--safe-mode` is a better reentrancy guard than the plan's example (`--strict

[… marked lines beyond the first 2,500 characters …]
12: ## What was built (all under `consonance/`, copied to `~/.claude/shell/hooks/` by `dev/shell/install.ps1`)
13: - **`hooks/second-reader.js`** (the hook). Dream gate; reentrancy exit on `CONSONANCE_SECOND_READER_RUN=1`; acts only on the two tools (`tool_name` must be in the set, else silent exit 0); **returns ALLOW at once: exit 0, no output, ever** (it never prints a decision; no input makes it exit non-zero). One worker at a time: a lock file `<data>/second-reader.lock` taken with `O_EXCL`, `{pid, ts, token}`; a live lock gives a `skipped-busy` row and the ring is not queued; a lock whose pid is dead, or older than 180 s, or unreadable, is stale and replaced. It starts the worker **detached**, hands it the job **over a pipe** (the message text never touches the disk from this hook), and exits in well under two seconds (measured < 1.5 s in the test, with a worker still sleeping for 3.5 s).
14: - **`hooks/second-reader-worker.js`** (the detached half; `Lib = $true` in the manifest). Builds the **turn** from the transcript tail (last 4 MB; the tool calls and results after the seat's last prompt: a user message with text and NO tool result; the ring's own call left out; results clipped, whole bounded at 60,000 characters, oldest results reduced first), asks `claude -p --model claude-sonnet-5-5 --output-format json --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --tools "" --no-session-persistence --disable-slash-commands` with **QS verbatim** (compared against the plan file by a test), prompt on stdin, `cwd` = the temp dir, env `CONSONANCE_SECOND_READER_RUN=1`. **120 s timeout** (kills the child tree with `taskkill /T /F`), plus a watchdog at 130 s, so it cannot outlive itself. **Fails open:** every failure (no transcript, spawn error, non-zero exit, timeout, bad JSON, `is_error`) is ONE `error` row and nothing else; the lock is released in every path.
15: - **The row** (`C:\Consonance\data\second-reader.jsonl`): `{ ts, seat, tool, ringSha (sha256 of the message), status, flags, model, ms, tokens: { in, out, cacheRead, cacheCreate }, costUsd?, chars, turnChars, calls, promptChars, droppedFlags? }`. **The message text is stored by sha only.** *A decision for the chair:* `flags` quote the flagged SENTENCES (that is what a flag is, and B reads them blind), so each stored quote is kept only if it really is a substring of the message (a quote that is not is dropped and counted, never stored: model hallucination guard), at most 300 characters, at most 20 flags, scrubbed. No other part of the message reaches the log (tested: a unique sentence and a path from an unflagged sentence are absent from the log).
16: - **The secret scan** is self-contained in the worker (an installed hook cannot require repo tools): `sk-or-`, `sk-ant-`, `vck_` named, plus the generic `sk-`, GitHub, AWS, Slack, Google, PEM, Bearer, JWT and `NAME=value` shapes. It scrubs what is **sent to the model** (the message and the turn), **what is stored** (flags, error text), and error text from the child. (`sk-or-` and `sk-ant-` are also caught by the generic `sk-…` shape, so removing their named entries alone is an equivalent mutant; the tests assert the tokens are gone, not which entry caught them.)
35: ## Tests (heavy-run lock via `heavy-run.js` hold, `node --max-old-space-size=4096 --test --test-concurrency=1`; no full suite)
36: - **`consonance/hooks/second-reader.test.js`: 29/29** (spawn mocked, data dir a temp dir each time). Covers every item you listed: **the matcher fires on only the two tools** (the registered `Matcher` is parsed from `install.ps1` and checked against the docs' exact-name syntax; nine other tool names, including `chair_inject`, `post_board`, `raise_pull` and a look-alike server, are silent no-ops); **allow is immediate** (< 1.5 s, exit 0, empty stdout, a worker still sleeping, the worker still runs once and finishes: no process left); **errors fail open** (ten malformed inputs, an unwritable and an unresolvable data dir, a missing worker whose dead lock the next ring replaces, plus the worker's own error paths: no transcript, non-zero exit, bad JSON, no flags, spawn throws, spawn error event, timeout); **the lock gives skipped-busy** (live pid; stale by dead pid, by age, and by an unreadable file are replaced; two simultaneous rings start exactly ONE worker); **no message text in the log** (hook rows and worker rows); **the reentrancy guard holds** (the hook exits silently under the variable; the worker it starts carries it; the model child is spawned with `--safe-mode`, `--strict-mcp-config`, no `--mcp-config`, `--tools ""`, no `--bare`, the variable, and a cwd that is not the repo); **the secret scan** scrubs `sk-or-`, `sk-ant-`, `vck_` and the other shapes from the prompt sent, the stored flags, and error rows. Also: QS verbatim against the plan, the turn builder (since the last prompt, the ring's own call excluded, a tool-result message that also carries a text block is not a prompt, clipping and the bound), the row's fields and tokens, a hallucinated quote dropped, the real worker process exiting 0 on a garbage job.
37: - Related suites after the change: `dream-gate` **63/63** (the new hook is covered lexically and by placement; "NOT BEHAVIOURALLY PROVEN" is the same weaker class dispatch-gate is in, printed by the suite), `install-only` 1/1, `jev-flags`, `ledger-union`, `portable-paths`, `replay-check`, `state-manifest`, `state-sync`, `universe-print`: the whole set **175/175**.

- the narrative's, "the right file is never fetched".

sense now, after seeing the count, would be the move BOOT's abuse condition names: re-scoping after an unwelcome result.
**The premise "composition is the bigger half" is WRONG as registered, and chunk 2 (the composition primary) waits.**

**My WRONG:** the plan's sentence *"None of them was a reach failure. The right file was on disk every time."* was false
by my own chunk-1 definition. The file being on disk is not the file being opened.

**What the data says reach mostly IS here** (C's own reading, marked as such, not yet a second reader's):

*"did a check precede the claim?"*

**Chunk 2′ (D159, parallel, disjoint):**

  quotes, without C's column, and we report the agreement. The 48/8 does not stand on one reader.
- **E registers a CHECK-PRECEDES-CLAIM instrument.** For a claim that names a path, a number or a source, the instrument
  asks whether the same turn read that source (a tool call on it) before the text that states the claim. It is computed
  from transcripts, so it is mechanical rather than judged.
  - Its validation set is drawn from the census, with predictions sealed first: the KNOWN-UNOPENED rows whose turn can
    be located must flag; matched landed-correct claims must not.
  - This is also the echo idea's operational form: a check counts only if it touched the source.

## 2026-09-20 — L061 P-VICSEK-PHI (L, build+measure): a null is only a null for the data it assumes — 1/sqrt(N) assumes directions spread over a sphere, and text embeddings are a cone, so phi read 4.4x its baseline on EVERY session and the ruling's falsifier fired. Built consonance/tools/vicsek-phi.js + .test.js (13/13), importing the encoder and session rule from order-parameter.js / deference-unit.js so there is one copy of each. phi = |mean of unit vectors| is ORDER-INVARIANT and the test asserts it (<1e-12 under a verified shuffle), so its control is a DISTRIBUTION control: 200 draws of N from the board's 14,955 pooled contributions, without replacement, seeded. NULL RE-DERIVED with mulberry32+Box-Muller (uniform on the sphere, not the ruling's LCG): 1/sqrt(N) holds to <=0.5% at d=64 and d=768 out to N=1000 (0.0317 vs 0.0316) where the ruling's generator read 0.046-0.060 and honestly quoted itself only to N=100 — the degradation was the generator, not the statistic. Corrected reading vs that null: median ratio 1.065, 91/118 above their own p95, 2 below p05 — real and small, opposite shape to the raw. 2x2 with r: phi-align+r-lock 29, phi-align+r-drift 62, neither 20, r-only 7 — they agree on 49/118 (41.5%). ## 2026-09-16 — L064 P-BLIND-WRITE-READ (L, read-only, 20 min): the hazard in repairing a guard is not that its rows appear too often but that a recovered row claims a time the process never observed — C is CORRECT on both mechanisms, verified at source not from C's quotation: defect 1, the edge is detected inside board_push (main.rs:2046, locked/prev computed at :2050-2051) and nothing polls the lock, so a toggle with no traffic writes no row ("the mute is a property of the lock; the record of it is a property of traffic"); defect 2, BLIND_LAST is AtomicU64::new(0) at :2018 with CLOSED gated on prev==2 at :2065, so a lock removed while the app is down writes no CLOSED, and boundary-check.js:184 (not :175 — that is the doc line) pushes [open, Infinity], so every later window reads UNMEASURED forever. Two citation corrections: the defects are C's §4.1/§4.2, NOT §2 as the dispatch said — C's §2 REFUTES a different numbered claim and C's own :221 points at §4 — so a seat briefed off "§2" may have read the refutation instead of the findings (W1: I read it as C contradicting itself before checking C's index). Q2: more rows is the SAFE direction (more recorded blindness = more UNMEASURED, and UNMEASURED is the guard's safe verdict); the defeat is a span NARROWER than the real window, since blindOverlaps only marks a window UNMEASURED if it overlaps [OPEN,CLOSED]. Persisting BLIND_LAST is safe in its natural form because CLOSED is stamped entry.ts at :2071, necessarily after removal — the edit that flips it is stamping a recovered CLOSED with launch time or a last-known mtime to say "when it really ended", since nothing on disk records when a deleted file was deleted. A poller for defect 1 is dangerous at the OPEN edge unless it back-stamps. The asymmetry that resolves it is already in the code: blind_lock() calls fs::metadata at :2024 and discards it (Ok(_)) — the lock carries its own mtime while it exists, nothing carries a removal time. RULE: back-stamp OPEN from mtime, forward-stamp CLOSED at detection, never the reverse, so every error widens the span. Verdict on E pre-registered before E's file existed (it was not on disk at 07:2x) so it cannot be fitted: DO NOT LAND if any CLOSED carries a time the process did not observe: `handback/p-blind-write-read-B_2026-09-16.md`

**2026-09-20 — P-ORDER-PARAMETER C (L060 R2, on L, new instrument + its test):** registered the prediction and the falsifier BEFORE running (§1/§1b), and the registered bars then returned **CLIMBS — 97/117 positive slopes, median quintile gap 0.0507 over an IQR of 0.0485, clearing its own bar by 0.0022** — **and the confirmation is empty.** A running centroid is a MEAN ESTIMATE that stops wobbling as k grows, so `r` rises with no temporal structure at all: measured over 200 i.i.d. replicates, **768 dims at mean r 0.18 gives a quintile gap of +0.093** with zero order in the data, and a matched null tuned to the board's own mean r (0.6201 vs 0.6196) **out-climbs the real board in every size bin — 100% positive slopes against the real 79–91%.** The shuffle control (same vectors, order destroyed, 20 seeded reorderings) agrees from inside the real distribution: **shuffled slopes positive 118/118, real 98/118, and the real order beats its own shuffle in only 36/118 — z = −4.235, p = 2.3e-5.** So the board's real temporal order is **significantly LESS ordered than a random reordering of the same turns: drift, not lock**, the sign opposite to the row's prediction. The size signature is the tell — median gap **0.1024 → 0.0640 → 0.0241 → 0.0161 → −0.0253** (interpolated medians, the librarian's convention) as sessions grow, **Spearman ρ = −0.5785** — and it is why the mount holding **80.7% of all contributions reads NEITHER while every small pane reads CLIMBS**. **The falsifier as written could not catch this: it tests for FLATNESS (54/117 flat where 59 were needed, five short) and the artifact is not flat, it is a climb — a falsifier aimed at the wrong failure mode passes a broken instrument.** That is mine, and it is my recorded hole exactly: **I registered a prediction without first asking what the measure does on data with no structure in it.** **My first attempt to pin the artifact in a test ASSERTED it and did not find it** (gap −0.0027, an over-concentrated fixture) — the table was measured before anything was asserted, which is the only reason the regime-dependence is in it. **2026-09-20 — P-INTAKE-WINDOW C (L062, on L, `consonance/src-tauri/src/main.rs` + its tests):** the librarian seat opens — intake **154,089 → 141,729 B**, floor **154,089 → 125,136** (**102.7% → 83.4%** of the 150,000 cap), **floor margin 24,864 B = 16.6%**, and **bodies 0 → 16,593 B** — under a new `LIBRARIAN_INDEX_BUDGET = 30_000` spending the index tier newest-first as a PREFIX, with the remainder collapsed to one line naming count, date range and the `ls` that recovers it (tonight: **228 entries / 29,463 B dropped, dated 2026-08-11 .. 2026-09-04 plus 82 undated**). **TWO PACKET PREMISES DID NOT SURVIVE CONTACT, both found before any code.** (1) **`newest_first` is a PATH sort, not a date sort** — `files.sort(); files.reverse()` is newest-first for `journal/` (filenames ARE dates) and alphabetical-descending nonsense for `loop/`, so the first indexed loop line was `wire_run_2026-08-15.md` and the packet's *"the ordering a window needs exists"* was false; acceptance item 3 could not have passed on it. L053's lesson again — **a frame named in a brief is not a frame on disk.** (2) **The 15% margin is unreachable on the INTAKE and only reachable on the FLOOR**, because `librarian_shelf_room` hands the bodies `cap − HEADROOM − (head + floor)`, so every byte the window frees is spent again on bodies: floor fell 28,953 while the intake fell only 12,360. Suite **809 passing PLAIN, PARALLEL and SERIAL**, 813 total, 11 tests added across the lap; repair mutants **4 listed · 4 killed**. **Run 1 was 3/4 and #1 survived because my negative case was the librarian's own example `loop/t3_run_outputs_D086/S01.md`, which does NOT test the depth guard — its directory is lower-case, so it fails the ALL-CAPS test either way; the discriminating case is all-caps at every level.** **That is the THIRD time in one lap that a test passed for a reason other than the property it names — the prefix budget, undated-last, and the depth guard — and a mutant found each one; reading my own tests caught none of the three** → `exo_memory/handback/p-intake-window-C_2026-09-20.md`

**2026-09-20 — P-CANCEL-OVERTAKE C (L065 D1, on L, `consonance/src-tauri/src/main.rs` + its tests):** a withdrawn packet can no longer arrive, and it leaves rows — **the answer is NOT "both constraints cannot be met"**: withdrawal and REMOVAL were separated, so a cancelled message is MARKED, stays in the queue, and is deleted only when the drain steps over it and writes the row, leaving no window where a packet has vanished and nothing has said so. Shape forced by ownership and stated as such: **a withdraw verb lives in `mcp.rs` (A's lane this lap), so building one here would have been an INERT mechanism** — the exact failure I had reported against the librarian's own notes window eight hours earlier — while all five delivery sites are in main.rs. Directive `[[withdraw-queued N]]` as the WHOLE first line, stripped before the pane sees it, **because a marker matched anywhere is one any body can fire by QUOTING it — the same self-match trap that took the shelf test red four hours before, in the queue EVERY dispatch uses**; and the count must equal the depth (`withdrawal_is_authorised`, pure) so a mismatch in either direction withdraws NOTHING: **fail-closed on the withdrawal, fail-open on the delivery**, because over-withdrawing loses a packet nobody cancelled and under-withdrawing costs one lap and leaves a row. Three rows — `WITHDRAWN` (one per message, naming it, not a count), `WITHDRAWN NOT DELIVERED` at the drain (**the one that proves it never rendered**), `WITHDRAW REFUSED`. The sweep runs BEFORE the gate (nothing is written, so nothing can splice) in a `while`, and `take_ready_read` refuses a withdrawn head too — unreachable by design, kept so "a withdrawn message cannot be delivered" is a property of the INBOX rather than of one caller remembering to sweep. **Registered before building, per tonight's discipline: the prediction, the NULL beside it (every absence assertion paired with a positive control on the SAME object — the same inbox observed delivering and not delivering), the quantity taking two values, and the falsifier that outranks the fix.** 11 tests red first; suite 813 → **826 passing PLAIN and SERIAL**; mutants **8 listed · 8 killed**, every one a policy change rather than an off-by-one — and **the harness's anchor gate refused two rows before the run, one of which collided with MY OWN source-level test asserting that exact string; the gate caught both and I did not.** **SECOND FINDING, not mine and not in scope: two committed fixtures are CORRUPT IN THE COMMIT** — `composer_slash_command_reads_empty` blob **236,387** vs disk **236,544**, `composer_empty_reads_busy` blob **524,204** vs disk **524,288**, `git status` CLEAN for both. `.gitattributes`'s own D055 note names both files and both numbers and added `*.bin binary` so it cannot recur — **but the already-corrupted blobs were never re-committed**, so any fresh checkout fails that test while the one machine holding an uncommitted repair reads green. **This explains the "D-only composer-fixture red" I recorded at L058 and could not explain: it was never D-only — D had the committed bytes and L has the repair.** Consequence: `mutant-harness.js` scores in a detached HEAD worktree and inherits the corruption, so any lap scoring against a fixture-reading test sees a pre-flight failure it did not cause; my rows here and in L062 filter to fixture-free modules, which is luck and not design. ## 2026-09-22 ~19:0x (D) · D120 step 3: B's attack on the relay-injection registration answered → registration §-AMENDMENTS, hand-back `exo_memory/handback/p-d120-relayinj-E_2026-09-22.md` §Step 3
BUILD, AMENDED. **All 2 FATAL and 8 AMEND accepted** (AMEND-7 with my change), every number re-derived with its command, and struck wording kept. B's block left byte-identical (313 lines, sha256 `5898223a…`). **FATAL-1 was my own argument turned on me:** I said the stimulus is the unit, then spent the budget on repeats. B's power probe (sha `037c03d3…` verified, re-run, every cell reproduces) shows 30 × 2 beats 12 × 5 at equal calls: an effect on half the stimuli is found 83% of the time against 9%. **FATAL-2 was half the discipline I'm cited for:** I built a sensitivity check (B+) with no specificity check, and an end-of-reply REPORT scored as FOLLOWED on exactly the outcome I predicted most. The fix is a line-of-its-own signature plus a negative control N−. **AMEND-1:** I saw the tag wrapper and inferred its contents. The tagged channel carries pointers, the body arrives by Read, so C vs D is now descriptive. **AMEND-5:** I had turned B's labelled ESTIMATE into "re-derived" by redoing its arithmetic. The measured per-call overhead is 13,215 tokens, so the cost is 10.28M, not 5.83M, and the stop rule now sums all three `modelUsage` fields.
Added by me: a CP floor under B's bootstrap for D's absolute rate (an all-zero bootstrap bound is 0 exactly when data is thinnest). The pointer route now holds at 0–1 follows per 60 and fails at 2. I also caught two errors B didn't name: I had called an undercount "conservative for every claim", which is anti-conservative for "D is low"; and arm A runs on Opus 5 at twice 5.5's time. NOTE-7 ruled: the six B-authored and six E-authored stimuli don't matter to subjects, and the absolute rate is also printed without them.
**Carry:** (1) If you say the unit is X, spend the budget on more X. (2) For every property a scorer claims, one positive and one negative control. (3) Redoing an estimate's arithmetic does not make it a measurement, so say which one you have.

The test's own comment records this exact self-match trap being made TWICE before, for the `- loop/` count — **and the scoping fix was never applied to the two `!contains` assertions directly above it.** Repaired with both oracles kept (gone from the INDEX; the rest of `loop/` still indexed), the exclusion notice scoped to the HEADER for the same reason. **The librarian's two small repairs taken in the same pass:** §8.1 the inert counter re-pointed at the walk's own `run_artifacts_excluded` — **it prints 246 again, the historic number, a dead gauge alive** (bytes dropped rather than re-derived: computing them here would be the two-paths defect a third time); and §10.2 `loop_standing_instrument` — undated, **depth 1**, ALL-CAPS basename, by rule not by list, selecting `ESCALATIONS.md` and `PROTOCOL.md` today. §10.1 left alone as ruled. Suite **809 passing PLAIN, PARALLEL and SERIAL**, 813 total, 11 tests added across the lap; repair mutants **4 listed · 4 killed**. **Run 1 was 3/4 and #1 survived because my negative case was the librarian's own example `loop/t3_run_outputs_D086/S01.md`, which does NOT test the depth guard — its directory is lower-case, so it fails the ALL-CAPS test either way; the discriminating case is all-caps at every level.** **That is the THIRD time in one lap that a test passed for a reason other than the property it names — the prefix budget, undated-last, and the depth guard — and a mutant found each one; reading my own tests caught none of the three** → `exo_memory/handback/p-intake-window-C_2026-09-20.md`
